# MIGRATION: trace of the deleted plans into planning/ and the docs (for the owner's review)

Work in progress on purpose: this file keeps the branch off `main` (`scripts/check_no_wip.py`) until the owner has read the three lists below and said
what happens to each item. Nothing here changes a subject file or a doc; the coordinator does that after the owner's answers.

**What was traced.** Every item of every file under the old plans folder at `127c80e8^` (the commit before the one that created `planning/`): 32 files,
**1699 items** (each table-row id such as `SC23`, `E17`, `DBR53b`, `QF6`, `I00`; each numbered decision row `#7`; each numbered decision, default,
follow-up or rule under a heading that says so; each second- and third-level heading). `python3 -I scripts/audit_plan_migration.py` re-reads the old
files from git and fails on any item without a row here.

**How to read a row.** Old file, the id or heading, a summary (the old row's own words, cut), the old status (from the old row: built, partly, open,
decided, parked, history; a heading of design is `design`), and the outcome: `task <ID>`, `design <SLUG> open designs`, `proposal <ID>`, `parked (index)`,
`doc <path>`, `built, documented (<path>)`, `superseded` (an owner decision recorded in the old plans closed it), `owner-dropped`, `history` (status
narrative, audits, milestones, cross references: nothing to carry), and the three markers the owner reviews: **PROPOSED DROP**, **THINNED**,
**CODE GONE, NEED REMAINS**. A fourth marker, **STALE**, is a task in `planning/` that plans work the old plans record as built and the code confirms.

**What was checked and how far.** Every open or partly built row was read in the old file and compared with the task or design it went to (the
"What", the "Done when" and the dependencies). Decision sections were checked against the doc the outcome names by searching for their key rules (a
rule not found is marked). "Built" was taken from the old row's status and spot-checked in the code where the outcome depends on it (the semantic
slices SL06, SL08, SL09, SL10, SL19; the service defaults of plan 12; `SyncEngines`; the certificate enroller's messages; the upgrade route). Rows marked
`built, documented` were not each re-verified against the code.

## Counts by outcome

| Outcome | Items |
| --- | --- |
| built (documented, or no doc needed) | 349 |
| doc (a decided rule or built behaviour described in a product doc; some also name CODECLEANUP-5 for the part not yet there) | 338 |
| task (a planning task carries it; 86 of these are decisions whose only carrier is CODECLEANUP-5, not yet in any doc) | 310 |
| cross-reference (a group heading, a decision heading or an `open-work.md` row whose items are traced in their own rows) | 238 |
| history (status narrative, audits, milestones, dependency and "waiting work" tables) | 179 |
| THINNED (carried, but the target lacks substance the old item had) | 131 |
| parked (index) | 61 |
| PROPOSED DROP (not carried anywhere; the owner decides) | 31 |
| superseded (closed by an owner decision recorded in the old plans: SC23, plan 35, plan 36, QG2) | 29 |
| proposal (a proposed decision in a subject file) | 13 |
| STALE (a task re-plans built work) | 9 |
| decided, nothing to carry | 4 |
| CODE GONE, NEED REMAINS | 3 |
| owner-dropped (dropped by the owner before the migration) | 2 |
| open design (CERTIFICATES) | 2 |

An item carries one outcome; a row that is both carried by a task and THINNED counts as THINNED. Many THINNED rows share one finding (all the MDX slices
share T1), so the lists below are shorter than the count.

## For the owner: PROPOSED DROP (not carried anywhere), by subject

Nothing below is dropped until the owner says so. Each says what it was, what happens if it is dropped, and a recommendation.

**CERTIFICATES**

- **X2. Renaming the console "DbDataKit Studio" and a separate admin tool named `dbdatakit`** (needs-review `27-studio...` sections 2 and 4, lane I
  `I02`, section 10's naming decisions, all dated 2026-11-02 as "decided"). The plan renamed the console's program to `dbdatakit-studio`, its folder,
  variables, `instances.yaml` to `servers.yaml` and the management API to version 2, so that `dbdatakit` could become a separate dotnet tool for
  administrators on their workstations. The new index calls this "overtaken" because `dbdatakit` is now the console's own command line
  (`docs/architecture.md` D3); the owner was not asked. Scenario: kept, an administrator types `dbdatakit-studio server list` on the console's host and
  `dbdatakit cert issue --server sync-prod` on a workstation; dropped, the console keeps its name and the workstation tool (CERTIFICATES' open design)
  needs another name or becomes a mode of `dbdatakit`. Recommendation: drop the rename; keep the workstation tool as the open design and name it there.
- **Trusting a company CA as a kind of trust-list entry** (`27-studio...` section 5, "Not in this plan"). Instead of one fingerprint per certificate, a
  server could be trusted for any certificate the company CA issues for its name, so an AD CS renewal needs no rollover at all. Scenario: the Gateway's
  certificate is renewed by AD CS every year; with a CA entry the console never notices, with fingerprints it must learn each new one (CERTIFICATES-6).
  Recommendation: keep as a line in CERTIFICATES' open designs.
- **The workstation tool's `doctor` ladder and the expired-console-certificate path** (`I12`, section 7.4). A command that walks configuration, reaching
  the console, its TLS state (trusted, untrusted with fingerprint, expired, wrong name, plain HTTP), sign-in and each server, stops at the first failure
  and prints the command to run on that host; and how the tool recovers when the console's own certificate has expired (show the fingerprint, accept
  `--trust`). `doctor` became `service check` for services (INSTALLER-1), but this ladder is about the workstation tool. Recommendation: fold into the
  open design "A command line that runs no server".

**INSTALLER and releases**

- **X1. 91 release-note entries written before the 2026-11-13 beta** (`RELEASE-NOTES-PENDING.md`, sections DbDataSync, DbDataBuild, DbDataStore,
  DbDataGateway, DbDataProxy, the console). The rebuild cut `docs/release-notes-pending.md` to changes since that beta, but the beta's GitHub release
  (`dbdatasync/v2026.11.13.1657-beta`) carries only the install text, not these notes, so they are in no release note. Several still matter to a person
  upgrading the Sync or Build command line from an older release: SQL Server and PostgreSQL connections verify the server certificate by default (the
  built-in `TrustServerCertificate=true` is gone); MySQL, MariaDB and Oracle are encrypted and verified by default; some engines' column types changed; a
  load that used to store a reinterpreted value now fails; script package hashes are pinned. Recommendation: restore the entries that describe a
  still-existing program into a "since the last stable release" section; drop those about the deleted servers and the Proxy (their deletion is already
  noted).
- **Upgrading an MSI-installed Windows service from the console** (24a `UC09`: built for folder installs, "Windows MSI apply not done"). Scenario: an
  administrator clicks Upgrade for a Runner installed by its MSI; today the console cannot apply it. Recommendation: a line in INSTALLER (after INSTALLER-22).
- **The cache screen's Playwright story** (24a `UC16`) and **naming a Gateway group's state group in `server.yaml`** (29a "Left over": the group is
  always `main`). Recommendation: drop both.

**MASTERDATA**

- **Entity roles read by the semantic layer** (15 "Not built" table and M7). `role: dimension | mapping | calendar` on a Store entity is parsed and
  validated and nothing reads it; the plan wanted the semantic layer to use such an entity as a governed dimension, value map, bridge or calendar.
  Scenario: stewards keep a `region_map` entity with `role: mapping`; with the feature a semantic query joining `NE` to `Northeast` uses it, without it
  the role is decoration. Recommendation: a task in MASTERDATA beside MASTERDATA-3.
- **The drafts' browser story and choosing a channel's draft in the console** (21 `CH01`; today a draft id is typed in the URL). Recommendation: a line
  in CHANNELS.

**Serving access rules (no subject owns them now; the nearest are SEMANTICS and MASTERDATA)**

- **What GP11, GP13 and GP14 left** (22a): the Store module's `/schema` route is not filtered by column rules (a hidden column's name is listed);
  saved-query and feed tests of their own; drawing a rules table in lineage; a screen to rotate the mask key; and the owner's deferred question whether the
  publish warning should also name a `null` rule on an Analysis Services column. Scenario for the first: a person with a `hide` rule on `salary` asks the
  Store's schema route and sees that `salary` exists. Recommendation: the `/schema` filter as a task (MASTERDATA), the rest drop.

**DATAQUALITY and ALERTING**

- **The setup checklist's git-remote step** (23a `FQ21`): BACKUPRESTORE-4 adds `git.remote` but not the checklist step that points to it.
  Recommendation: a line on BACKUPRESTORE-4.

**KNOWLEDGE**

- **The command-spec golden for every product** (27a `AG01`: kept for the console only; a golden of offered MCP tools per product catches a command that
  starts offering secrets to an agent). Recommendation: a task (small).
- **The workspace overlay** (27a `KN18`): a back-end agent working on a branch reads that branch's knowledge files over the published ones, so it sees
  the instruction it is editing. Recommendation: a line on KNOWLEDGE-6.
- **SMB with Kerberos proven on a Windows host** (27a `IX05`) and **a semantic query as an outbound source of the integration kit** (27a `IX22`).
  Recommendation: drop the second; the first as a check by hand on KNOWLEDGE-23.

**SUBSCRIPTIONS**

- **The report-catalog snapshot carries the report server's address** (28c `QE23`, so the Gateway can build the link without a connection).
  Recommendation: a line on SUBSCRIPTIONS-6.

**PAGEFORMAT**

- **Wall-mode rotation by URL** (`?rotate=a,b&every=60`, owner decision 11 of plan 32; 32 `DBR08`), with jitter and the refresh floor as server
  settings. Scenario: a TV in the warehouse cycles three dashboards. Recommendation: a task (S) in PAGEFORMAT.
- **PDF link annotations and the bundled icon font with its pin** (32 `DBR37`, `DBR51`): a `print: link` element is a clickable link in the PDF; icon
  cells print with a pinned font. Recommendation: a task in PAGEFORMAT.

**PUBLISHING**

- **"Not now" items of plan 30**: a reproducible-build comparison as a console action, and an approval chain across several groups. Recommendation: drop.

**Repository conventions**

- **The old index's cross-cutting conventions** (old README): SemVer per package, public API baselines, a changelog per package, a hub `contract.yml`.
  None is the repository's practice. Recommendation: drop.
- **Fabric through the Gateway** (`open-work.md`, Build row, `F1`, "needs a tenant"). Recommendation: a parked line.

## For the owner: THINNED (carried, but the target lost substance), by subject

Each says what is missing and a proposed fix.

**SEMANTICS**

- **T1. The MDX, XMLA and Postgres-wire design** (14 R7, R8; 14a section 7, SL40 to SL55, D5, D6, D7, D32; 20 O16, O17). The index keeps one line. Lost:
  translate only the MDX Excel sends into semantic requests, never a general MDX engine, refusing anything else with a clear XMLA error; Excel's recorded
  traffic on a Windows VM is the specification and the CI fixture; the order (Discover rowsets, then rows, columns and totals, then filters and
  drill-down, then member search); model additions (an `All` member, measure groups, display folders, format strings); XMLA over HTTPS with Windows or a
  personal access token; Postgres wire answers semantic SQL only, every member has a flat column name, `pg_catalog` is emulated tool by tool (psql and
  a Python client first, then Power BI); Quack waits for a DuckDB server specification. `docs/semantic/architecture.md` says this is "written down" in
  `planning/SEMANTICS.md`, which does not hold it. Fix: an "Open designs" section in SEMANTICS (or `docs/semantic/protocols.md`) with this text.
- **An agent's Gateway address for Build's `semantic` noun** (14a J15): `DbDataBuild/DESIGN.md` says it is "open, plan 33 SC23 in the plan"; no task
  holds it. Fix: a proposed decision in SEMANTICS.
- **Browser stories written and never run** (14a SL12 to SL31's explorer story, 22a GP35 and GP35d, 24a UC13's Link flow, 27a AG24, 32 DBR45 and others):
  they are listed in `docs/ui-coverage.md` but no task runs them in CI, except AUTHORING-9 for the Workspace. Fix: one task per host (console, Gateway)
  like AUTHORING-9.

**KNOWLEDGE**

- **T2. The knowledge design of plan 27 section 2** (2.2, 2.6, 2.7, 2.9 to 2.11 and decisions 35 to 58). The tasks KNOWLEDGE-2 to KNOWLEDGE-11 are one
  line each; lost are the decided details: the module checks and embeds at staging and switches at activation; generated items are references, one per
  subject, refreshed per channel; column values only by opt-in under the column's rules; verified queries are found by the question, run directly or
  adapted, are the front-end agents' evaluation cases, and are marked "needs re-verification" without blocking the model; the knowledge app's pages and
  the `@dbdatakit/knowledge-ui` components; one search box asks several sources and groups results. `docs/knowledge.md` points to "plan 27 section 2",
  which is deleted. Fix: move the unbuilt design into KNOWLEDGE "Open designs" or `docs/knowledge.md`.
- **T3. Actions and elevated actions** (27 sections 4.4 and 4.5, decision 11, 27a AX01 to AX12, SD10, SD18, SD20; confirmed by the owner 2026-11-03 and
  2026-11-04). Lost: an action is one typed operation (input and output schemas, an effect class); an elevated action has its own principal per channel,
  granted entities, operations, fields and row conditions, approved by an administrator **and** the data's owner; people get *run* on it; the action
  validates the caller's request and applies or rejects it; the four worked examples (an expense claim moved from Draft to Submitted by its requester
  only, and so on); elevated writes in a non-production channel go to the preview's draft layer. Fix: as T2.

**MASTERDATA**

- **T4. Documents and WebDAV** (15 M5; 20 O09, O15). Lost: files are stored in the database in chunks, on the model's engines; every save is a version;
  WebDAV for Excel with an open-source library only (Dav.AspNetCore.Server first, NWebDav second, fixed in a fork rather than switching to a commercial
  one); Excel's WebDAV traffic recorded on the Excel VM as fixtures; Office sign-in by MS-OFBA to the home's page. Fix: an open design (MASTERDATA, or a
  new open-design subject for the parked lines).
- **Extraction on save and promotion** (15 M6, M8; 25 section 3). Lost: a saved extraction spec is checked against each new version's fingerprint, a moved
  layout is reported and the last good extraction kept, extracted tables land in a Store entity; a temporary value map an agent made for a query can be
  promoted into a Store entity and the query keeps working ("the main collaboration path between people and agents"); an agent proposes the map as a
  Store draft and the person approves each step. Fix: as T4.
- **The Store's approval rule** (15 Q4, owner 2026-11-09): change requests are not required, approving your own request is not blocked (the Changes
  screen says "You proposed this request"), and a model that wants a second person says `approval: owner`. It is in no doc. Fix: a paragraph in
  `DbDataStore/README.md`.
- **The store module's remainders** (20 O08b): a model's instance copies are not served, a token's scope narrowing is not read, and writes are not a
  change set. MASTERDATA-4 names instance-scoped tokens only. Fix: lines on MASTERDATA-4.

**CERTIFICATES**

- **The decided parts of the workstation tool reduced to open questions** (lane I `I07` to `I09`, `27-studio...` 6.1 to 6.5). The open design asks
  "how it signs in (a personal access token or a device-code approval)"; the old plan had decided it: device-code sign-in (`/api/auth/cli/start` and
  `poll`, an approval page showing the code, the label, the requesting address and the console's fingerprint; 10-minute codes; rate limits; a `local`
  method approved on the host and a `windows` method), the session cached protected, exit code 3 when not signed in, `dbdatakit.yml` found up the
  directory tree with a user-level file, trust on first use for the console's certificate. Fix: write these into the open design as its starting point,
  marked as decided on 2026-11-02 under the old name.
- **Trust-list details** (`I01`, section 5): the server screen shows which entry was seen last and when each was added; a record with a single
  `fingerprint:` is refused with "pair again"; docs say "trust", not "pin". Fix: add to CERTIFICATES-5's "Done when".
- **Discarding a staged certificate** (section 7.3, `DELETE /api/v1/certificate/stage`). Fix: add to CERTIFICATES-4.
- **The CA facts** (section 3): the new CA accepts only RPC/DCOM enrollment and publishes no CES; the legacy CA's CES answers 503 and the server may be
  retired; so only a domain-joined Windows host can request today. Fix: one paragraph in CERTIFICATES "What exists".
- **The risks** (section 9): the key passes through the console in memory; device-code consent phishing; an unattended renewal has no service identity;
  a pending request keeps an encrypted key on the workstation up to 14 days. Fix: add to the open design's questions.

**INSTALLER**

- **T13. PrivateTmp hides a Kerberos ticket cache in `/tmp`** (12 follow-up 1). The shared unit generator applies the full sandbox to the four services;
  a Linux Runner whose tasks use integrated authentication would not see a ticket cache in `/tmp`. Fix: a line in `docs/install.md` or the Systemd README.
- **Two defaults left out of INSTALLER-P1** (36 defaults 5 and 7): a service that finds its own log subfolder or failure file owned by another account
  writes nothing there and says so in the event log; an identity home running on its own keeps a port in the console's sub-block. Fix: add to INSTALLER-P1.
- **`release.json` with the compatibility generation** (24a `UC08`): the release workflows do not write it, so "outside N-1" shows only for a mirror.
  Fix: a line on INSTALLER-29.

**APPHOSTING**

- **Identity home remainders** (20 `O06b`): `dbdatakit identity run` does not offer the upstream sign-ins, and a group of identity homes needs its
  data-protection folder copied instead of protecting its signing keys with the group key. Fix: lines on APPHOSTING-8 (or ENDPOINTS-4).

**ENDPOINTS**

- **Traces** (22a `GP29`): people's ids on spans and `traceparent` on the console's status and pairing calls. Fix: add to ENDPOINTS-5.

**TRUSTZONES**

- **Who may start a task from a view: three layers** (28c `QE19`): the app's access rules show the link, the app manifest lists the tasks it may name
  (`runner:`), and the Runner's grant says which client may start which task. TRUSTZONES-5 names only the grants. Fix: add to TRUSTZONES-5.

**DATAQUALITY and PUBLISHING**

- **What a block stops** (23a `FQ17`): the console's Quality page counts the nodes downstream; it should list the dependents a block refuses or holds.
  Fix: a line on DATAQUALITY-5.
- **Refusing a rules table no published Build model produces** (22a `GP02`): the check waits for `build-metadata` (KNOWLEDGE-4) and today only warns.

**PAGEFORMAT**

- **The export component's unrun parts** (32 `DBR14` to `DBR16`): the pinned-browser run, the Windows leg, the font jobs, `page.pdf()` goldens, the browser
  process's memory limit. PAGEFORMAT-12 says only "proved on a real release". Fix: list them in its "Done when".
- **Smaller remainders** (32 `DBR17`, `DBR19`, `DBR27`, `DBR28`, `DBR29`): the dashboard snapshot's layout choice and SVG golden; the `frame-src` policy for

**AUTHORING**

- **The catalog cache's producer** (33 `SC20j`, decision D20): a paired server holding the logins answers read-only catalog calls and the answer is

**Product docs and CODECLEANUP**

- **The format read-forward rule** (33 section 6d and D17, owner 2026-11-10): a kind reads `Previous` to `Current`, an old file is not checked for new

- **Decisions marked `task CODECLEANUP-5`** (170 rows of 14, 14a, 22a, 23a, 24a, 27, 27a, 28c and 32 name it; 86 have no other carrier): decided rules that are in no product doc yet,

- **Plan 07's three rules** (reader-shaped interchange stays the default; MySQL, Oracle, SCD2, Snapshot and segmenting move only when a second product
  needs them; Sync adopts the write gate first). Fix: a sentence in the Transfer README's "Parked" paragraph.
- **RDL without a report server** (old README "Parked"): Majorsilence refuses matrices, gauges, sparklines, maps and `Lookup`, so a sample of real reports
  must be tried first. Fix: add to the index line.

## For the owner: CODE GONE, NEED REMAINS

- **Sync's engine lists from the registry** (14a `SL04`). Sync read the engine registry's `sync_source` and `sync_target` roles in the Sync server
  (`SyncEngines` in `DbDataSync.Api`), deleted with it. Whether the Workspace's sync project forms offer only the engines the registry supports was not
  checked. Scenario: an author picks MySQL as a Sync target; with the registry the form offers it with its status, without it the list is whatever the form
  hard-codes. Proposed owner subject: AUTHORING.
- **The Build editor of a model's `queries:`** (14a `SL20`): built on the Build server and deleted with it. Carried by AUTHORING-5 (no action needed
  beyond confirming).
- **The back-end agent's workspace** (27a `AG14`): built in a Build server workspace, deleted. Carried by AUTHORING-8.

Also gone with the servers and already carried: the enroller's message naming `dbdatasync config cert retrieve` (CERTIFICATES-1), evaluation runs on the
console's embedded runner (KNOWLEDGE-16), Build's publish of semantic models (the Workspace publishes).

## Also found: STALE tasks (planning re-plans built work)

- **SEMANTICS-2, SEMANTICS-3, SEMANTICS-4 and SEMANTICS-5** plan the mapped-column check (14a SL06: `MappedColumns.cs` in the Gateway), types in `describe`
  (SL08: the `type` column of `DescribeAnswer`), decimals as `decimal128` with the source's scale (SL09, D33: `ArrowRows.cs`) and the semantic models in
  Build's metadata with a relationship's origin (SL10: `origin` and `declared_at` in `SemanticShowCommand.cs`). All four are built. What is left of them is
  SL06's SQL Server leg, never run. Fix: rewrite SEMANTICS-2 as that leg and remove SEMANTICS-3 to SEMANTICS-5 (by a proposed decision, as ROLES.md asks).
- **SEMANTICS-7** restates a built promote (the Gateway's `SavedQueriesPage`); what is left is the paste into a Workspace project, after AUTHORING-5.

## Process findings: what the audit showed to be unsafe

- **P1. Nothing checks substance.** `check_plans.py` checks ids, slugs, references and links; it cannot see that an owner-decided design became one
  line. The migration itself was done without a trace, so losses (T1 to T4, X1, X2) went unnoticed until the owner found one. This audit script covers
  the one migration; a later rewrite of a subject file has the same risk. Suggestion: a rule in ROLES.md that a change which removes design text from a
  subject file or a product doc names where the text went, and the coordinator checks it like a removed proposal.
- **P2. CODECLEANUP-5 lets an agent drop decisions.** It says "drop what later work overturned", which contradicts ROLES.md ("nothing is ever dropped
  from planning without the owner saying so"); and it lists 8 of the old files, while 07, 15, 33 (section 6d) and the needs-review proposal also hold
  decisions found in no doc. Suggestion: CODECLEANUP-5 lists each entry it does not carry as a proposed decision, and covers all files of this trace.
- **P3. The cleanup tasks remove the last pointers.** `docs/knowledge.md` ("Designed in plan 27 section 2") and `docs/semantic/architecture.md` (MDX
  "written down" in SEMANTICS.md) point at designs that exist only in git. CODECLEANUP-6 to CODECLEANUP-11 remove plan references; done before T1 to T4
  are fixed, nothing in the repository says the designs exist. Suggestion: CODECLEANUP-7 onward depend on the owner's answers to this trace.
- **P4. Parked lines hold no design.** ROLES.md keeps unscheduled designs under "Open designs" of a subject, but the index's "Parked" section is one line
  each and is maintained by the coordinator, so a parked design has no home (MDX, documents and WebDAV, backlog, custom attributes, the Excel add-in, RDL).
  Suggestion: a subject with status "open design" for parked work (for example PARKED.md), one heading per parked line.
- **P5. Judgments in the index.** The index line for the Studio proposal says "overtaken", and CERTIFICATES says the `doctor` command is "overtaken":
  judgments the owner did not make, written by the coordinator, who may only maintain order, status and assignment. ROLES.md asks for a proposed decision
  first, but nothing enforces it for the index. Suggestion: the coordinator's rule says an index line never states a judgment; a parked or overtaken item
  cites the owner's words or is a proposal.
- **P6. Shared files were rewritten, not appended to.** ROLES.md says `docs/release-notes-pending.md` is edited by adding entries; the migration replaced
  it and the 91 older entries are in no release note (X1). Nothing checks append-only edits of shared files. Suggestion: a check, or a coordinator rule
  that a removed entry in a shared file is listed in the pull request with where it went.
- **P7. "What exists today" was not checked against the old statuses.** Four SEMANTICS tasks re-plan built slices because the old rows' "built" status
  was not read. Suggestion: a subject agent's first step checks each task against the code before starting.
- **P8. The coming id check will flag this audit.** CODECLEANUP-6 refuses references to old plan ids in code, docs and scripts;
  `scripts/audit_plan_migration.py` and this trace are made of them. When the owner signs off, delete both (the trace is wip and must go before `main`
  anyway) or exempt them in CODECLEANUP-6's baseline.

## The trace

Grouped by old file. Status is read from the old row. Long outcomes repeat for each item of a group so that each row stands alone.
